TM 11-2146/TO 16-80         TCC7-15,  6 October
                                                 1954, is changed as follows:

                                      ‘CHAPTER 1

                                      INTRODUCTION

      Note. (Added) The basic manual covers Manual Telephone Switchboard SB-249/TTC, serial numbers 1 through 321,
and Trunk Relay Telephone Circuit TA-226/TTC, serial numbers 1 through 107. Manual Telephone Switchboard
SB-249/TTC, serial numbers 322 through 780, is similar to the switchboard described in the basic manual except for
circuit modifications that permit dialing without the use of a special dial cord and that also permit ringing on the answer
cords. Trunk Relay Telephone Circuit TA-226/TTC, serial numbers 108 through 260, is similar to the trunk relay case
described in the basic manual except for circuit modifications that permit dialing without the use of a special dial cord.
All information in the technical manual applies equally to Manual Telephone Switchboard SB-249/TTC, serial numbers
322 through 780, and Trunk Relay Telephone Circuit TA-226/TTC, serial numbers 108 through 260, unless otherwise
indicated in this change. In this change, for purposes of simplicity, the serial numbers are used to identify the different
models of SB-249/TTC and TA-226/TTC as shown below:

1 through 3821 represents   Manual Telephone             1 through 107 represents                        Trunk Relay Telephone

Section Il. DESCRIPTION AND DATA

 3. Purpose and Use                                          4. System Application

   Manual Telephone Central Office AN/TTC-7                      a. T'wo-panel Multiple. Normally, the AN/TTC-7
                                                             serves 200 common battery (CB) or CB-LB lines
 (fig. 1) is a complete transportable telephone ex-          and 20 trunks. The lines are displayed on a two-
 change. It is designed for use at any headquarters          panel basis; each panel is multipled to corre-
requiring telephone facilities. The AN/TTC-7 can             sponding panels on the other two switchboard
be installed and put into operation rapidly, and             positions. When the number of lines served exceeds
can be dismantled and moved in a relatively short            200, but is less than 400, the two-panel multiple
time.                                                        may still be used with three additional positions.
                                                             In this way, a six-position, two-panel multiple can
    a. Three common battery switchboard positions            accommodate 400 lines. Since additional cables
and relay equipment for 200 lines and 20 trunks,             (CX-2584/U (8 feet long)) are needed for this
with the necessary power equipment, are provided             arrangement, it is advisable to use the four-panel
for an initial installation. This equipment, which is        multiple whenever two or more Central Offices
displayed on a two-panel multiple basis, is covered          AN/TTC-7 are combined.
in detail in this manual.
                                                                b. Four-panel Multiple. When the number of
   b. Each switchboard position is equipped with             lines exceeds 400, a four-panel multiple should be
15 cord circuits. This permits 45 simultaneous calls.        used. In a four-panel multiple, the line and trunk
                                                             units are connected to positions 1 and 2. The
   c. The face equipment of each switchboard                 panels on position 1 are then multipled to corre-
position is factory-wired to accommodate 500 lines           sponding panels on all the odd-numbered positions
and 80 trunks. The extra facilities in the face equip-       in the exchange; the panels on position 2 are multi-
ment are provided for the normal growth and                  pled to corresponding panels on all the even-
expansion of the exchange. For each 200-line addi-           numbered positions. In any case, the number of
tion, three more switchboard positions are re-               positions used in the exchange will depend on the
quired. If more than 400 lines are to be accommo-            number of lines served and the traffic rate. Each
dated, the multiple should be planned on a four              additional 200 lines requires three additional
panel basis. On this four panel basis, the exchange          switchboard positions for optimum -service; an
can be expanded to accommodate 1,000 lines and               office serving 1,000 lines will usually consist of 15
160 trunks. Additional cables will be required               positions, or three positions for each 200 lines.
whenever 2 or more Central Offices AN/TTC-7
are combined.                                                   c. Trunking. The lines that terminate in the
                                                             AN/TTC-7 are equipped with local or common
   Note. The bottom group on the right panel of each posi-   battery telephones. A typical trunking diagram is
tion is reserved for the future addition of trunk            illustrated in figure 2.
circuits that are not supplied with the central office. The
bottom group on the left panel of each position consists of
common battery-local battery (CB-LB) line jacks equipped
with designation strips. The manual or dial trunk circuits
provided at this time will terminate in this group.

as illustrated in figure 37. Figure 38 illustrates a
typical manner of connecting ten incoming lines
to T and R binding posts at the line side.

                        CHAPTER 6

       SHIPMENT AND LIMITED STORAGE
AND DEMOLITION TO PREVENT ENEMY USE

Section |. SHIPMENT AND LIMITED STORAGE

80. Disassembly                                        f. Place all the accessory equipment, such as
                                                   the operator’s headsets and message registers,
   Disassemble the equipment as follows:           in the wooden box (Accessories Case CY-
                                                   1499/T).
   a. Disconnect and unplug all cables and cords
and remove the outside lines from the binding          g. Place the three operator’s chairs in the
posts on the main distributing frames.             three wooden boxes (Chair Case CY-1490/T).

   b. Coil the cables and cords neatly and pack        h. Place the covers on the four battery cases
them in the wooden boxes (Cable Assembly Case       (Case CS-63) and latch them securely.
CY-1498/T).
                                                   81. Repacking for Shipment or Limited
   c. Replace the covers on the switchboards and         Storage
Panel BD-132-A, and latch them securely.
                                                       The AN/TTC-7 needs no further packing for
   d. Replace the front and back covers of the     shipment or limited storage. When in transit or
MDF case and the line and trunk relay cases,       in storage, cover the components with the duck
and latch them securely in place.                  tarpaulins provided for protection against the
                                                   weather, dust, and dirt.
   e. Remove the cases from the base stands and
fold up the base stands.

Section Il. DEMOLITION OF MATERIEL
         TO PREVENT ENEMY USE

82. General                                           c. Burn. Burn cords, resistors, capacitors, wir-
                                                   ing, and technical manuals; use gasoline, kero-
   The demolition procedure outlined in para-      sene, oil, flame throwers, or incendiary gre-
graph 83 will be used to prevent the enemy from    nades.
using or salvaging this equipment. Demolition
of the equipment will be accomplished only upon    d. Bend. Bend panels and cabinets.
order of the commander.
                                                      e. Explode. If explosives are necessary, use
83. Methods of Destruction                         firearms, grenades, or TNT.

   a. Smash. Smash switches, controls, capaci-        f. Dispose. Bury or scatter     the destroyed
tors, and transformers; use sledges, axes, pick-   parts in slit trenches, fox holes  or other holes,
axes, hammers, crowbars, or other heavy tools.     or throw them into streams.

   b. Cut. Cut all cords, cables, and wiring; use  g. Destroy. Destroy everything.
axes, handaxes, or machetes.
